## §2 Decisions in force
- D-01 · 2026-08-02 · No medical claims, treatment or surgery content — YMYL rejection risk (Raptive + search raters).
- D-02 · 2026-08 · GA4 wired through direct `gtag`, no GTM — GTM is a Raptive integration step, not an approval requirement; adds a per-site console we don't need.
- D-03 · 2026-08-13 · Before Git-connecting any Vercel project, `origin/main` must match local HEAD — the near-miss would have redeployed the fabricated build.
- D-04 · 2026-08-15 · No visible "Last reviewed" date; schema `dateModified` stays — the visible label implies a review cadence that doesn't exist.
- D-05 · 2026-09-30 · Consent through Raptive's CMP only; no second consent tool.
- D-06 · 2026-10 · Option B: no calorie, protein, sleep or exercise targets of our own — only official guidelines, quoted, attributed and linked.
- D-08 · 2026-10 · Goal: every page perfect (all 40, not only new ones), before the final round.
- D-09 · 2026-10 · Design bar for data and tool pages: real interactive US map (not a tile grid), results that explain the differences and stand out, beautiful inputs, charts where nothing overlaps.
- D-10 · 2026-10-07 · Every number on a page traces to the verified registry (`research/data-sources.csv`) or our own reproducible calculation — unsourced claims are cut, not hedged.
- D-11 · 2026-10-07 · ACE (fitness-industry) body-fat tiers banned; age- or sex-specific "healthy BMI ranges" banned — adult cut-offs are the same for everyone at every age.
- D-12 · 2026-10-11 · Remove visible dates from bylines and article meta on every content page ("Reviewed <date>", "Updated <date>", "<Month YYYY> • N min read"). Schema `dateModified` stays (D-04). Data-provenance dates stay (survey periods, "CDC released/updated …", "retrieved <month>"). Scope: 22 content pages — 7 show "Reviewed <date>" (e.g. `/average-weight/` "Reviewed October 4, 2026", `/bmi-chart/` "Reviewed 27 September 2026"), 2 show "Updated October 7, 2026" (`/blog/what-is-bmi/`, `/blog/bmi-for-athletes/`), 13 older guides show "February 2026 • N min read" (6 of them also show "Updated February 2026"). Resolves the earlier "Reviewed/Updated" byline open question. Legal pages `/privacy/` and `/terms/` keep their "Last updated" line (owner, 2026-10-12).
- D-13 · 2026-10-12 · Action colour: text links and action buttons (`.calculate-btn`, `.aw-cta`) use cyan-700 `#0e7490` (`--link`), hover cyan-800 `#155e75` (`--link-hover`). No gradient, glow or lift on buttons. Brand cyan `#06b6d4` stays for accents, borders and chart fills. Owner decision (option A of three in chat's render).
- D-14 · 2026-10-12 · The CMB-006 page pattern is the standard for every page: tool first on tool pages; answer block and key takeaways; contents chips; a worked example; real tables and charts; flat FAQ (JSON-LD = visible); sources and method; one related block; one 680 px reading column. Each page gets its own wedge and data, never a template copy (google-ai-content-policy). Owner decision ("much much better").
- D-15 · 2026-10-22 · `bodyfatpercentagecalc.com` (owner's other site) and `calculatemybmi.net` both ranking for body-fat queries is the goal (owner: "if we have #1 and #2 position, that would be superb"). The sites stay standalone (no cross-links), and their pages stay distinct.
- D-16 · 2026-10-22 · Raptive content-ad placement: owner's call "lets presume raptive knows what it is doing, no need to correct them." No request goes to Raptive about placing content ads inside the `<article>` wrapper. Rebuilt D-14 pages ship without the single `<article>` wrapper (sections sit directly in `main .container`, as on the calculators and `/blog/bmi-chart-men/`); other wrapper pages change only in their own rebuilds.
- D-18 · 2026-11-05 · Stage 4 is done because the site is on Raptive: keep policy pages true and don't break ad placement. (Its Stage 2 six-page limit ended with D-26.)
- D-19 · 2026-11-06 · Replaces D-07. IndexNow runs for the rebuilt pages now and again at the end. Request indexing in Search Console only after asking Marko (D-28), highest ledger volume first, each URL logged in §5. Why: 13 of 40 pages had zero impressions and `/average-weight/` had never been crawled, so holding submission until all 40 are done costs more than it saves.
- D-20 · 2026-11-06 · New pages, `/average-height/` included, wait until the uncrawled pages are crawled. Why: a page Google doesn't crawl earns nothing whatever its ledger volume, and the page map marks the `/average-height/` head terms as likely zero-click. (Its rebuild order gave way to D-26 on 2026-11-13.)
- D-22 · 2026-11-07 · /ideal-weight/ calculator: Hamwi in his published pounds (100 lb + 5 lb per inch over 5 feet for women, 106 lb + 6 lb for men); frame size changes Hamwi only (±10%, his rule in Davidson 2015 Table 2); no formula number under 5′0″ (the formulas start at 60 inches, Peterson 2016); the span of the four replaces the average. Why: the metric Hamwi printed 1 lb under his own rule, the old code applied his frame rule to three equations with no frame term, and the average was our construct.
- D-23 · 2026-11-11 · /kids-bmi-calculator/: every age uses CDC's half-month row (2 years 0 months is the 24.5 row, not 24.0), and a severe-obesity result names AAP's class 2 or class 3 as CDC quotes them. Why: CDC's data file page says each half-month row covers the whole month.
- D-24 · 2026-11-13 · Replaces D-17. Chat does the work itself in its own shell: no prompts for Marko to paste. Each change goes on `claude/cmb-NNN` with a PR, and chat merges it (merge commit) once every test passed and it has looked at the changed pages in Marko's Chrome on the Vercel preview at desktop and phone width, every tool on them used once. Right after, chat confirms Vercel's production build of the merge is READY and checks the live page, and reverts the merge if the build failed or the page is wrong. No Chrome: the change waits for Marko. Why: Marko's rule, 2026-11-13; it replaces the lines in this repo and in site-pipeline where the owner merges or pastes prompts.
- D-25 · 2026-11-13 · Replaces D-21. Pages are checked on the Vercel preview. Live pages get Chrome only as a last pass, on a few pages, because the site shows live Raptive ads. Desktop and phone width are same-origin frames inside the preview tab: 1280 × 800, and 390, 360 and 320 px; touch and iPhone Safari stay unverified. Why: Marko, 2026-11-13.
- D-26 · 2026-11-13 · Goal: the most Google traffic, and three scores at pass on every page. S sourcing: every number comes from a verified source and nothing is invented (D-10). P prose: anti-slop-prose, with prose-score's 2.0 tells per 1,000 words as the floor. D design: Impeccable, 0 detector findings at 390 and 1280 px (results open on tool pages) and no page wider than the screen. The Raptive score is out of this run; ads stay safe (D-18). `research/page-scores.csv` from `scripts/page_scores.py` is the scoreboard, and its Scores line tops §0 in place of site-pipeline's Stages block. After Marko's "go": the remaining number mismatches first, then down the list ten pages at a time; merge by D-24, rebuild the list, update this file, and carry on until something needs Marko. Why: Marko, 2026-11-13.
- D-27 · 2026-11-13 · No three-looks (page-looks) run: keep the design the site has (CLAUDE.md R2). Why: Marko, 2026-11-13.
- D-28 · 2026-11-13 · Ask Marko before touching: the URL, title or H1 of a page that gets traffic (any Search Console clicks or impressions; only §0's zero-impression pages are free); ad placement; the author wording rules (§1 Author, D-04, D-12); Vercel settings (never change them); Marko's nine sites (this chat works in this repo only). A page that can't pass without breaking one of these goes on a list for Marko. Search Console is read only after asking. Why: Marko, 2026-11-13.
- D-29 · 2026-11-13 · The repo stays public: nothing private goes in DEVLOG.md, page text or commit messages. Settles the repo-visibility question open since 2026-10-10. Why: Marko, 2026-11-13.
- D-30 · 2026-11-13 · A BMI stated at a weight the page prints is the BMI of that printed weight, in the unit shown; round once (§4) still holds for figures derived from estimates. Why: `/ideal-weight/` printed 183 lbs next to BMI 26.2 (from Hamwi's unrounded 182.6 lb), and 183 lb typed into the site's own BMI calculator gives 26.3.

## §3 Rejected — don't re-propose
- 2026-08-02 · Medical / treatment / surgery content — YMYL rejection risk.
- 2026-08 · Tag Manager (GTM) alongside direct gtag — Raptive overhead with no site benefit.
- 2026-08-15 · Visible "Last reviewed <date>" byline — implies a review cadence that doesn't exist.
- 2026-09-30 · Second consent tool alongside Raptive's CMP — consent conflicts, duplicate consoles.
- 2026-10 · Our own calorie / protein / sleep / exercise targets — hedges data integrity; official guidelines only.
- 2026-10-07 · ACE body-fat tiers and age/sex-specific "healthy BMI ranges" — not supported by CDC/WHO cut-offs.
- 2026-10-11 · Visible "Reviewed <date>" / "Updated <date>" / "<Month YYYY> • N min read" bylines — implied a review cadence we don't run (D-04/D-12); schema `dateModified` carries the review record.
- 2026-11-06 · Resubmitting the sitemap: Google read it on 2026-11-05, so it adds nothing.
- 2026-11-07 · An adjusted body weight calculator (3 KWP groups, about 12,600 searches a month): it is a drug-dosing weight, out under D-01.
- 2026-11-11 · Kids page sections on health conditions, causes, activity and sleep guidance and "when to talk to your doctor": out under D-01 and D-06; the tool's job is the percentile.
- 2026-11-13 · A three-looks (page-looks) run to restyle the site: Marko keeps the design the site has (D-27).

## §4 Gotchas & hard rules
- Pages are minified onto one line — `grep -c` counts lines, not matches. Use `grep -o PATTERN FILE | wc -l` in gates.
- Editing visible FAQ text can break or desync FAQPage JSON-LD; re-validate with `python3 research/f1/verify.py` after any FAQ change.
- The jsdom test scripts under `research/` need Node + jsdom; the box has Node 20 but no jsdom (install step missing by design) — they ship for reference and run on Marko's Mac. Treat skipped jsdom runs as unverified.
- Content batches ride in `research/<batch>/`; only their `apply_build.py` writes files. Never edit a batch-managed file by hand mid-flow — the hash-guarded replacements will refuse the next run.
- The box has no browser. Cloud Claude Code sessions (D-17) have Chromium and Node Playwright 1.63.0 in `/opt/pw-browsers`; `pip install playwright==1.63.0` adds the matching Python binding (never `playwright install`), so the repo's Python browser checks run there, and the impeccable launcher fetches its pinned engine (`scripts/VERSION`, 0.1.14) into `~/.impeccable/bin/` once, checksum-checked, which is not an update (R5). Layout, hover and viewport-specific behaviour stay `unverified` until chat's Claude in Chrome check (D-21: the Vercel preview before the merge, the live page after it); there is no owner phone test and no Mac detector run.
- `scripts/check_structure.py` enforces balanced tags through `scripts/check_markup.py` since CMB-003 — a batch whose patch drops a closing tag fails the 40/40 gate. Use `scripts/check_markup.py --text FILE` before vs after to prove which text a batch changed.
- `scripts/check_structure.py` finds the repo from `__file__` and gates label wiring since CMB-023.
- Balanced tags can still be nested wrong. Since CMB-004 `scripts/check_markup.py` adds three structure checks — `block-in-heading`, `outside-container`, `content-in-calculator` — all run from the 40/40 gate.
- Markup and layout changes need a render check; the box has no browser, so chat renders a pushed branch. Patch whole elements, never text that runs across tag boundaries — that is how the 21 CMB-003 pages broke.
- One click binding per FAQ accordion and per mobile-menu toggle. Two bindings cancel out (one tap opens and closes) — the CMB-005 regression on 13 pages. Since CMB-005 `scripts/check_structure.py` fails any page whose FAQ or menu toggle has a click-binding count ≠ 1.
- FAQPage JSON-LD answers must equal the visible answers on the same page. `scripts/check_structure.py` enforces this since CMB-005; keep the schema in step whenever FAQ text is rewritten.
- `assets/js/calculator.js` rounds BMI once via `round1()` — categories, what-if tables and BMI Prime all come from the shown value. Healthy limits are whole units whose rounded BMI falls inside 18.5–24.9 (`weightBounds()`), the rule the BMI charts use. A page table that gives kg for an inch height labels the cm with one decimal (5'10" = 177.8 cm).
- Page dates follow git history (since CMB-009): schema `dateModified` = the last commit that changed the visible text inside `<main>`; `sitemap.xml` `lastmod` = the last commit that changed that text, its links or the JSON-LD. Removing or editing a visible date moves neither. Dataset objects keep their own `dateModified` tracking the data. Check with `python3 research/dates/date_truth.py` (exit 0 = every page agrees); run it after every batch's commit.
- `date_truth` reads git history, so a shallow clone gives false BAD lines: run `git fetch --depth=400 origin main` first. A squash merge on a later day makes it fail, a merge commit passes (chat simulated both on 2026-11-05): merge with Create a merge commit.
- Since CMB-009 `scripts/check_structure.py` fails any page whose byline shows a review/update date and any "N min read" line (D-12). Legal pages `/privacy/` and `/terms/` keep their "Last updated" line.
- Since D-17 each `claude/ID` branch carries its own DEVLOG commit and is merged before the next branch starts, so `DEVLOG.md` doesn't conflict; if two branches are ever open at once, merge it per the devlog skill (every §6 entry in date order, §0 rebuilt). Replaces the CMB-006 rule that pilot branches never carry DEVLOG changes.
- Weight-by-height tables list the lowest to highest whole lb and whole kg whose rounded BMI falls in the category at that exact height. Each unit is worked out on its own, never converted from the other. A paired height label has one decimal (5'2" = 157.5 cm; 145 cm = 4'9.1"). Since CMB-008 `scripts/check_weight_tables.py`, run by `check_structure`, enforces range cells (`A–B`, `≤`, `≥`, `<`) in any table with a BMI-category header. Single-value cells (min/max columns) are not gate-checked — build them with `research/chart-tables/chart_rules.py`. A cell that reads as a weight pair (`5.1 lbs (2.3 kg)`, no "~") in a table whose headings say BMI makes its row a weight row, so an inch height there needs its cm to one decimal (CMB-031, `/blog/how-to-lower-bmi/`).
- Since CMB-010 `scripts/check_content_integrity.py` (imported by `check_structure.py`) fails: a call to action without a link; empty boxes / steps / table frames; cut sentences; duplicate or dead related links, or cards inside a paragraph; emoji in visible text; inline-styled button links or bylines; D-11 sex-specific ranges; placeholders in headings (`(teaser)`, TODO, TBD). Pre-CMB-010 score 5/40.
- The impeccable detector cannot see a missing link, a cut sentence, an empty box or the calculator results panel. Before every batch, chat runs the `research/site-checks/` probes (`text_probe.py`, `box_probe.py`, `ui_probe.py`, `overflow_menu.py`) and `snap_results.py` for the results-panel findings. Detector runs cover a calculator page only *before* Calculate.
- Shared component rules (DESIGN.md has the details): `.cta-button` / `.cta-button--sm` for links that act as buttons (flat `--link`, D-13); strong category shades for bars and steps that carry white text, unlabeled bars keep the base colours; category table cells use the `/bmi-chart/` tints with text tinted from the same hue; cards and FAQ items inside a section render flat (no card-in-card); no emoji or pictograph icons anywhere, including calculator results. Since CMB-011 `.related-grid` / `.related-card` is a flat link list (divided by hairlines), used by `/blog/` and `/404.html`. `.cat-dot` is the category dot in static tables, in the strong shade of the result card's palette.
- Calculator results rules (DESIGN.md "Calculator results — answer first", since CMB-011): flat tint card with the BMI number in the strong shade; sentence-case `<h2>` sections divided by hairlines (no card-in-card); right-aligned tabular numbers with the other unit sitting under each value; category colour only on dots and BMI values; the user's row gets a "You" pill (never an arrow glyph); the user's unit shows first everywhere; metric BMI is `kg/m²`, US units keep 703; no own targets, no unsourced thresholds. Result category names follow CDC: Healthy weight, Overweight, Class 1 / Class 2 / Class 3 obesity; WHO's Severe and Moderate thinness below BMI 17.
- Since CMB-011 `scripts/check_structure.py` runs `js_problems()` on `assets/js/calculator.js` (no emoji / arrow entities, no low-contrast hexes as text colour, no gradients).
- Since CMB-011 the content-integrity gate (`scripts/check_content_integrity.py`) also fails: numbered items that skip a number (`#2` missing between `#1` and `#3`, or `Method 2` alone); a caption for a missing table ("Rows…/Columns…" without a table, or "the table below/above" with none); empty chart rows / icons / bar tracks; headings with nothing under them.
- Since CMB-011 `check_structure` fails `/blog/` unless it links every sitemap guide and its `ItemList` and visible counts (title, meta, intro) match. A new guide must be added to the hub in the same batch.
- A gate cannot judge every leftover of a bulk deletion ("This paradox…" with the paradox gone). After any bulk-deletion commit, run `python3 research/site-checks/residue_scan.py <commit>` and read every hit — CMB-011 found 15 leftovers on 9 pages this way.
- Since CMB-012 `apply_build.py` substitutes `{TODAY}` in an edit's new text with the box's local date (the same clock `git commit` uses). Batches that write sitemap `lastmod` and schema `dateModified` use this, so `date_truth` passes on any day as long as apply and commit happen the same day. `CMB_TODAY` env var overrides it for tests only.
- US rank rule (since CMB-012, on `/` and `/men-bmi-calculator/`): "higher than X%" counts only adults with a strictly lower BMI (read the NHANES curve at BMI − 0.1); "lower than Y%" counts only those with a strictly higher one. Equal BMIs count on neither side. The old rule double-counted ties and added 0.5–1 point.
- In calculator results the converted unit shows one decimal both ways ("72 kg (158.7 lbs)", "159 lbs (72.1 kg)"), so a converted healthy limit never rounds across a category line. Applies to healthy-limit tiles, ideal-weight BMI-range tile, milestones and the weight tile.
- Per-page browser checks (each docstring lists its checks and arguments; Playwright + Chromium, which cloud sessions have, D-17): `/` `research/home-build/home_test_playwright.py`; `/blog/healthy-bmi-range/` `research/healthy-range-build/hbr_test_playwright.py`; `/blog/bmi-chart-women/` `research/chart-women-build/bcw_test_playwright.py`; `/bmi-chart/` `research/bmi-chart-build/bc_test_playwright.py`; `/body-fat-percentage-chart/` `research/body-fat-chart-build/bfpc_test_playwright.py`; `/women-bmi-calculator/` `research/women-calc-build/wbc_test_playwright.py`; `/blog/bmi-chart-men/` `research/chart-men-build/bcm_test_playwright.py`; `/average-weight/` `research/average-weight-build/aw_test_playwright.py`; `/body-fat-calculator/` `research/body-fat-calculator-build/bfcalc_test_playwright.py`; `/blog/how-to-measure-body-fat/` `research/how-to-measure-body-fat-build/htm_test_playwright.py`; `/us-obesity-statistics/` `research/us-obesity-statistics-build/obs_test_playwright.py`; `/new-bmi-calculator/` `research/new-bmi-build/nb_test_playwright.py` (and `nb_breaks.py`: 5 deliberate breaks on a temp copy that must each fail); `/calculators/` `research/cmb-027/hub_examples_playwright.py` (its nine examples typed into the calculators they link to, so run it after a change to any of those calculators too; `hub_breaks.py`: 24 deliberate breaks that must each fail); `/ideal-weight/` `research/ideal-weight-build/iw_test_playwright.py` (and `iw_breaks.py`: 9 breaks); `/kids-bmi-calculator/` `research/kids-build/kids_test_playwright.py` (and `kids_breaks.py`: 7 breaks). The 25 pages Stage 1 hadn't covered: `research/stage1-audit/audit.py BASE [--pages /a/,/b/] [--out DIR]` (CMB-029, every number, report only; `audit_breaks.py`: 3 planted errors; since CMB-031 a C number with no link, manual row or named author tries the page's own page-map `data_source` row and its dataset at the printed precision (`data/cdc/bmi-age-2022.csv` keyed by sex, birthday row and column) before the pages_served fallback); without `--out` it overwrites the committed report, and its `manual.json` keys hand classifications to a sentence's wording without its numbers, so a reworded sentence leaves its entry unused (printed) and its numbers unclassified (exit 1) until the entry is updated; an A check also holds the category word its sentence states, so renaming a category in a worked example means changing its entry (CMB-031). It takes a table's source from the first element after it that starts "Source:", "Data" or "Note" (a registry link there names the row for every cell) and reads a FAQ question alone, so a number in a fixed FAQ question needs its own entry (CMB-030). Run a page's check after any change to that page. The check counts and break counts of each build are in its §6 entry. `calc_e2e_playwright.py` runs at 390 px only.
- Since CMB-013, `apply_build.py` also substitutes `{TODAY}` in whole new/replaced files whose manifest entry has `"today": true`. The hash is of the package copy (which keeps `{TODAY}`); the written file carries the apply date. Pages ship as templates this way for schema `dateModified` and `article:modified_time`. The sitemap `lastmod` stays a `{TODAY}` edit.
- NHANES comparison rule (since CMB-013): a page states a difference between groups only when its 95% CI excludes 0 (Taylor linearisation, SDMVSTRA × SDMVPSU, 15 df). Otherwise it gives the margin of error. For NHANES 2021–2023, a difference between NHANES 2021–2023 estimates uses 2.13 × SE (t at 95% for the survey's 15 design degrees of freedom), not 2 (CMB-022 fact-check; no claim fell between the two).
- Guide-page order (DESIGN.md "Guide pages (CMB-013)"): H1 + byline → 3-paragraph intro → key takeaways (answer first) → disclaimer → hero figure → chips → sections → FAQ → sources & method → one related block. In `main.reading` the takeaways answer and list have `max-width: 34rem` (≈75 characters a line).
- A "see a doctor" line needs an official source that says when (e.g. MedlinePlus unexplained-weight-loss). No bare lines.
- `.weight-chart` (DESIGN.md, CMB-014/CMB-015) is the chart-table component for D-14 chart pages: category tints with text tinted from the same hue, white hairlines between cells, 8 px (0.5rem) beside the text (CMB-015 bump — the detector asks for 13 px text), the height column kept in view while the body scrolls sideways on phones, `#h-F-I` row-anchor links, `min-width: 600px` inside `.table-responsive`. Since CMB-015 `.th-sub` also works in `.data-table` row headers.
- Printable chart on a chart page: an `<img>` in `figure.figure.figure--wide` with alt text, width and height, linked to the full-size file so image search can index it. Its cell values ship as a CSV next to the generator, and the page's browser test checks every one.
- Attribute values never hold a raw `"`: write heights with primes (`4′10″`) in `meta`, `og:*` and `twitter:*` content attributes — the raw quote closes the attribute and search engines / social cards show a truncated description. (CMB-014 fixed this on `/blog/bmi-chart-women/`, `/bmi-chart/`, `/blog/bmi-chart-men/`.)
- Images in search (DESIGN.md "Images in search results (CMB-015)"): every page carries `<meta name="robots" content="max-image-preview:large">`. Each D-14 page names a text-free preferred image (its own figure, no words, numbers or logo) in 16:9 1200×675, 4:3 1200×900 and 1:1 1200×1200 from `research/preferred-images/pref_images.py`: `og:image` and `twitter:image` = 16:9 (`og:image:height` 675), the main entity's JSON-LD `image` = [1:1, 4:3, 16:9], a WebPage `primaryImageOfPage` = 16:9. A figure meant for image results is an `<img>` (inline SVG is not indexed as an image) with alt, width and height, listed under its page in `sitemap.xml` as `<image:image>`. `scripts/check_images.py` (run by `check_structure`) enforces all three; a D-14 rebuild adds its page to `PREFERRED` there in the same batch.
- Body fat rank tool and lookup tables (DESIGN.md "Body fat rank tool and lookup tables (CMB-016)"): the tool uses the shared calculator parts (no page-level style); results answer-first with a neutral number (a share is not a BMI); `h3` headings inside a section that has its own `h2` use `.er-section h3`; shares never print as 100% or 0% ("more than 99%", "under 1%"); halves round up in tables and tool alike; three-column chart tables use `.weight-chart.weight-chart--fit` (no 600 px minimum, cells may wrap) and show the range as tint plus a second line; women `#be185d` and men `#4338ca` as chart series.
- Round once (since CMB-016): every printed figure is rounded from the unrounded estimate, never from a stored rounded value. Stats files store enough decimals and stop if storing would change a printed figure (`bfpc_stats.py keep()`). The CMB-016 fact-check caught 28.35 printing as 28.4 (unrounded 28.347) and shares stored at 0.1 printing a point high.
- `research/body-fat/` is the B1 build record: `compute_bf.py` still feeds `research/body-fat/bf_results.json` (quoted on `/`, `/blog/healthy-bmi-range/`, `/blog/bmi-chart-women/`, `/bmi-chart/`, `/age-bmi-calculator/`, `/women-bmi-calculator/` and `/men-bmi-calculator/`), but `gen_bf.py` there would rebuild the pre-CMB-016 page; the current `/body-fat-percentage-chart/` generator is `research/body-fat-chart-build/gen_bfpc.py`.
- Waist field and result boxes (DESIGN.md "Waist field and result boxes (CMB-017)"): the optional waist input beside the weight; up to three flat `.rank-box` sections after the shared answer-first panel (rank, "US women with a BMI like yours", waist check), each hidden when it has nothing to say; the waist check compares what it prints (waist to 0.1 in or 0.1 cm; the ratio worked out from that waist and cut, never rounded up, to two decimals, so 0.50 means at least half the height; BMI as shown); the US-guidelines line compares in the unit entered and names only that unit; the ADA sentences quote the waist in cm against 88 cm; "for women of non-Asian background" leads when the Asian-background answer differs; the source line says these are published thresholds applied to the numbers entered, not a diagnosis.
- Distributions over the chart (DESIGN.md "Distributions over the chart (CMB-018)"): a chart figure can show where real people fall on it — per-height rows over the light category tints, a 6 px bar in the series color for the middle half and a 3 px ink tick for the median; box plots with a 10th–90th line, a 16% middle-half box, an ink median tick and a dashed reference line drawn per row; row labels above their box.
- Cross-page numbers (since CMB-018, broadened in CMB-019): `research/average-weight/avgw_results.json` feeds `/average-weight/` and the men's chart quartiles (`bcm_stats.py` asserts them equal); `/bmi-chart/`, `/blog/bmi-chart-women/`, `/women-bmi-calculator/` and `/age-bmi-calculator/` also print its means. A change to `research/average-weight/compute.py` regenerates every page that prints a changed value in the same batch.
- D-14 pages put their sections directly in `main .container`, no single `<article>` wrapper (D-16). The calculators and `/blog/bmi-chart-men/` already did this; CMB-019 drops the wrapper on `/average-weight/`; other wrapper pages change only in their own rebuilds, not in a sweep.
- `.form-row` sets `display: grid`, which beats the plain `hidden` attribute: a tool that hides form rows with `hidden` needs a scoped `[hidden] { display: none; }` (CMB-019 `.awt [hidden]`).
- `.data-table--fit` (DESIGN.md, since CMB-019): five-column number tables fit a 390 px phone (13 px type, 6 px side padding below 600 px, numbers on one line). Use rather than per-page table CSS. Six number columns add `.data-table--tight` (4 px beside the text below 400 px; CMB-028); when the headings are names that must not break, `.data-table--small-head` sets them at 11 px below 440 px, bottom-aligned, 2 px beside the text below 380 px (CMB-031), which fits `/ideal-weight/` from 360 px up with whole names (327 px needed at 13 px against a 280 px frame); no soft hyphens there.
- Average weight tool (DESIGN.md "Average weight tool (CMB-019)"): the tool uses the shared calculator parts; the answer-first card is tinted by BMI category only when a weight is entered; the CMB-018 box plot is drawn in the unit entered; a height with fewer than 30 measured gets no average; by-height tables carry row ids and a † under 60 measured.
- Body fat calculator (DESIGN.md "Body fat calculator (CMB-020)"): four methods on the shared calculator parts, one pane at a time; the answer-first card is tinted by the Gallagher provisional range (neutral under 20); every method says how close it is likely to be (CDC's scans where the survey measured the inputs, a published DXA study where it didn't) and what that check can't tell; the CMB-016 rank figure is reused; another method counts as an "other estimate" only once typed into (height and weight whenever a weight is entered).
- A unit switch converts to 0.1 and restores the entered numbers when switched back with nothing retyped, so flipping units never changes a result (CMB-020 `.bfc`; whole centimeters had moved 33.0% to 32.9%).
- Exact ties: when the inputs are recorded to fixed decimals (BMI and DXA to 0.1, age whole), round a computed estimate and its error to that exact precision before comparing (within 3, window bounds); float noise otherwise decides the ties (CMB-020 fact-check FAIL — Deurenberg hundredths).
- Percentiles of DXA readings: use the weighted midpoint rule over distinct values (`research/body-fat-calculator-build/bfc_stats.py`); the rule can't depend on the order of tied readings. A CDF interpolated between distinct values runs about 0.07 low on readings recorded to 0.1.
- Tool figures keep every label inside the SVG: draw marks from `y+2` in an 88-unit-tall figure (CMB-019 `/average-weight/`, CMB-020 `/body-fat-calculator/` and `/body-fat-percentage-chart/`). The generators' label checks include the figure's edges.
- Below 600 px a table heading row that spans its columns (`th[colspan]`) gets 8 px beside its text (`styles.css`, `.flow .data-table--fit th[colspan]`); the detector flags 6 px as cramped.
- `research/body-fat-calculator-build/bfc_stats.py` asserts its sample equals `bfpc_stats.json` (10,864 adults, means 38.71 / 27.24): a change to the chart's sample reruns the calculator's check in the same batch. CDC's raw DXA files stay out of the repo (registry `nhanes-2011-2018-dxa`).
- Tables whose row labels are names of several words take `.data-table--fit.data-table--wrap` (DESIGN.md "Tables with long row labels (CMB-021)"; `styles.css` `.flow .data-table--wrap th[scope="row"] { white-space: normal; }`). `.flow` tables otherwise keep row labels on one line, so both CMB-021 tables overflowed a 320 px phone without it. Groups inside a table are `<tbody>` elements led by `th[scope="rowgroup"][colspan]`; a number keeps its unit on one line (no-break space).
- A published comparison table is typed into a JSON file in the build folder (e.g. `research/how-to-measure-body-fat-build/burns_2019_table3.json`) that the generator, the preferred-image drawing and the page test all read; the page test types the values in again from the paper, so a changed file fails it.
- Burns 2019 Table 3 is "DXA – surrogate": every value is positive, so every method read lower than DXA on average. Write "read X points below DXA", never "within X points" or "off by X"; the average miss (MAPE, a share of the scan's reading) is the magnitude. Frija-Masson 2021's "median absolute error" is a signed kg difference (scale minus DXA): fat mass −2.2 to −4.4 kg means the scales read low; for weight write "median weight differences of 0 to 0.3 kg", never "within 0.3 kg".
- The average miss as a share of the scan (MAPE) depends on the average reading: compare MAPEs only within one sample, and across samples give the miss in points (CMB-021 fact-check: CDC's scans average 32.9% body fat, Burns's students 27.0%).
- NCHS surveys named: the 1960–1962 survey is NHES I (NCHS joined CDC in 1987); when the hero and CSV span 1960–2023, call the long series "CDC's national series (NHES, NHANES)", not "every CDC survey". Early surveys ran "two years or more", up to six for NHANES III 1988–1994, never "about two years".
- Quoting other definitions or other surveys on an obesity data page: next to each figure state who was counted and how (weighed or asked; years and ages; the definition), and give the BMI-only figure for the same people where the study gives one (Fourman 2025 42.9%, Al-Roub 2025 39.9%). Never set those figures against CDC's measured rate as a trend — they count different people under different rules.
- `.data-table--fit.data-table--wrap` under 360 px keeps 4 px beside the text (`styles.css`); long single-word headers carry a soft hyphen. Lets four-column number tables fit a 320 px phone (the two CMB-022 and the two CMB-021 tables overflowed otherwise).
- A rebuild of a page regenerates its `number_audit.py` allow list (`research/us-obesity-build/tools/allowed_numbers.txt` since CMB-022) from the verified page itself — never add numbers to the allow list one at a time, which hides unsourced figures. The audit scans `<main>` (the old `<article class="cmb-stats">` wrapper is gone).
- `/new-bmi-calculator/` (DESIGN.md "New BMI page (CMB-023)"): `research/new-bmi-build/gen_nb.py` owns `<main>` and the head's title (since CMB-024), description, OG/Twitter tags and JSON-LD and rebuilds them from `nb_stats.json` (`nb_stats.py` = `calculateNewBMI()`'s arithmetic: round once, difference = the two rounded values subtracted); change the generator, never the page by hand. `/ideal-weight/` works the same way: `research/ideal-weight-build/gen_iw.py` from `iw_stats.json` (`iw_stats.py` = the ideal-weight functions' arithmetic, every printed value checked in exact fractions). `/kids-bmi-calculator/` too: `research/kids-build/gen_kids.py` from `kids_stats.json` (`kids_stats.py` stops unless bmi-age-2022.csv's SHA-256 matches CDC's file and `assets/js/cdc-bmi-lms.js` holds every row; that copy keeps sigma and P95 exactly and L, M and S at 8 significant digits, which changes no printed value).
- Exact halves: lb × 703 ÷ in² can land exactly on x.x5 (5′0″ and 180 lb = 35.15, printed 35.2 because `round1()` adds 1e-9); check a rational BMI against its exact fraction rounded half up and keep the near-edge guard for New BMI, which is irrational (`nb_stats.py` `f1()`). The label gate counts input, select and textarea as fields (the embed code on `/us-obesity-statistics/` is a labelled textarea). No-break spaces between numbers and units stay out of SVG text (it never wraps; tests compare labels with plain spaces). `toFixed(1)` prints a decimal half stored just below it a step low (Miller's 77.35 kg for a 6′3″ man: 77.3), so the ideal-weight display rounds halves up with `round1()`'s 1e-9 nudge (CMB-028).
- "Peak" rule: a series' highest value is a "peak" only when the later estimates are *significantly* lower; otherwise "highest estimate". A share that moved within a range is described by that range (overweight 30.3% to 34.4%), not by its first and last values.
- Keyword volume on a page Google hasn't crawled earns nothing. Check Search Console before ranking work by ledger volume.
- prose-score counts the hidden `—` placeholders in calculator results as em dashes. `/new-bmi-calculator/` reads 2.2 per 1,000 words for that reason alone (chat, 2026-11-07). Page length is the page tests' count: `textContent` of `<main>` without svg, script, style and the results panel (New BMI 1,861, `/ideal-weight/` 2,035); the generators' printed counts split table cells and run higher.
- A page can dip after a rebuild: `/men-bmi-calculator/` sat near position 45 on Oct 13–20 and was back at 11–18 from Oct 22.
- Prose-only batches (since CMB-026): guard them the way `research/cmb-026/prose_guard.py` does (every number in text and attribute values, links, titles, meta tags, h1–h6, table cells, FAQ questions and the JSON-LD compared with the base, FAQ answers and `dateModified` masked, the allowed cuts applied to the base) and prove the guard with breaks (`guard_breaks.py`); for another page set, copy it and change `PAGES` and the cuts. A vague authority whose claim carries a number can't be cut in such a pass (numbers stay): name a source already on the page only after reading that it says so, otherwise log it for Stage 1. prose-score doesn't count spaced hyphens (" - ") used as dashes, but they are the same tell; in formulas and formula cells (Mifflin-St Jeor, Harris-Benedict) " - " is a minus sign and stays.
- Robinson's 1983 abstract gives 51.65 kg + 1.85 kg (men) and 48.67 kg + 1.65 kg (women) per inch over 5 feet. The 52 + 1.9 and 49 + 1.7 in common use are Pai and Paloucek's rounded table values; the site uses them and says so. Pai and Paloucek's 10% is "for individual differences" in the rule behind Devine's equations. The frame ±10% is Hamwi's (Davidson 2015 Table 2, citing Hamwi 1964).
- PMC shows a reCAPTCHA to fetchers. Chat reads PMC articles in Chrome.
- Keyword Planner returns no data for kids and child BMI phrasings ("bmi calculator for kids", "child bmi calculator", "kids bmi calculator"); their demand shows only in Search Console.
- Claude in Chrome's tab window is fixed at 606 × 667 here (resize_window reports success and nothing changes). Check widths in same-origin iframes inside the preview tab, scaled to fit (D-25); a javascript_tool call times out at 45 s, so measure one or two pages per call. Previews show Raptive's privacy dialog: choose Reject All.
- Only `gen_nb.py`, `gen_iw.py` and `gen_kids.py` still rebuild their pages byte for byte (checked 2026-11-13). The other `gen_*.py` were build-time patchers that assert an older head (og:image:height 630) and stop; never run them on today's pages.
- `python3 scripts/page_scores.py --qa ../portfolio-qa` rewrites `research/page-scores.csv` (about 10 minutes, no tokens; portfolio-qa needs `npm ci`); `--pages /a/,/b/` re-measures a batch and keeps the other rows; `--devlog` writes §0's Scores line. Delete a row of `research/page-scores-known.csv` in the batch that fixes it. The Stage 1 audit isn't calibrated for the 15 page-test pages: a trial on 2026-11-13 gave 444 unclassified, 3,549 C with no row and 103 mismatches there, nearly all misreads of dataset values and written-out arithmetic.
- Rank curves (since CMB-033): `research/rank-curves/curves.py RAW_DIR OUT` computes the six curves (adults, men, women, women 20–39, 40–59, 60+) at 4 decimals from the NHANES 2021–2023 files in the repo and stops unless they round to the old 2-decimal curves (`curves_2dec.json`) and 4 decimals change no displayed rank; `apply_pages.py` wrote them into `/`, the men's and the women's page scripts; `rank_check_playwright.py BASE` types every changed BMI into the three calculators. `home_stats.py` and `men_stats.py` store 4 decimals and reproduce their JSON; `wbc_stats.py` stops at its decade check (`avgw_results.json` changed in CMB-019), so its JSON's curves were patched from `curves.json`. `.chart-range` (also CMB-033) is a BMI scale from 15 to 45 drawn to scale: ticks and markers at (BMI − 15) / 30 of the width, zone names in `.chart-range-legend`; `/age-bmi-calculator/`'s bars set their own inline gradients and don't use it.
- Menu marker (since CMB-034, `research/cmb-034/menu_marker.py`, enforced by `check_structure.py`): a header link to the page itself carries class "active" and aria-current="page" (never the logo); a page the menu doesn't link marks its section with aria-current="true" (Guides for guides, Obesity Statistics for the state and childhood pages); other pages mark nothing. A page whose old pattern copied another page's header must be re-run through the script.

- What: applied `research/cmb-012-home/` — the homepage `/` rebuilt to the D-14 pattern. **Why**: `/` owns the biggest keyword cluster on the site (2,240,000/mo for "bmi calculator" close variants; GSC baseline average position 44.6, 126 impressions, 7 clicks in 7 days). The old page was pre-D-14 template — Key Takeaways inside the calculator card, inputs without `for`/`id`, "When to see a doctor" with unsourced thresholds, pros/cons table with no sources, "research is ongoing" dead-end, build-note in link grid, WebSite JSON-LD `SearchAction` for a search the site doesn't have; detector 20 at 390 / 48 at 1280. Owner's 2026-10-14 SERP pastes show no top-10 result places the reader among US adults — that is the wedge. **Tool first**: H1 + two-line hook + the calculator with `for`/`id` inputs; Calculate button bottom at 770 px on 390×844 (above fold); scale labels use CDC names (Under / Healthy / Over / Class 1 / Class 2+). **The rank wedge**: after Calculate a rank box shows "Higher than X% of US adults" against the 28.2 median, plus a sentence for men only and women only — CDC NHANES Aug 2021–Aug 2023, 5,929 adults aged 20+, survey-weighted, pregnant women excluded (reproduces NCHS Series 3 No. 50 medians 28.2/28.3 and means 29.4/30.0 and the crude NCHS prevalences 1.6/32.1/40.3/9.4). **Below the tool**: 55-word answer with disclaimer and contents chips → "How to read your result" worked example (5′7″, 160 lb, BMI 25.1; "one pound over the line" figure) → US-adults compared percentile table (all / men / women + 72.4% stat) → adult categories with share of US adults + 100-square figure → healthy weight by six heights in lb and kg each worked out on its own → BMI by hand (US, metric, cm formula) → when BMI misleads (muscle, waist, age, Asian ancestry WHO 2004, 2025 Lancet Commission) → children & teens percentile table → health (NHLBI + Wing 2011 + Health E-Stat 119) → six situations → FAQ → sources & method → related. **FAQ**: 8 questions, the 4 PAA from the SERP pastes first, then normal BMI / men vs women / "Is BMI a percentage?" / accuracy; JSON-LD equals visible text. **Head**: new meta/OG/Twitter descriptions; new OG image `assets/images/og/home-bmi-us-adults.png`; `WebApplication` described; `SearchAction` removed; `<title>` unchanged (P3 stays open). **Rank rule tightened** on `/` and `/men-bmi-calculator/`: "higher than X%" counts only strictly-lower BMIs, "lower than Y%" strictly-higher; equal BMIs count on neither side. Men example 5′10″ 180 lb (BMI 25.8) moves from 32% → 31%. **Shared results panel** (`assets/js/calculator.js`): converted unit shows one decimal both ways ("72 kg (158.7 lbs)" ⇄ "159 lbs (72.1 kg)"), so a converted limit never rounds across a category line — applies to healthy-limit tiles, ideal-weight BMI-range tile, milestones and the weight tile; milestone column says "−1 lb" not "−1 lbs". **Fact-check fixes** (independent agent): `/men-bmi-calculator/` Winter 2014 "found the lowest death rates across BMI 23 to 33" → "found that the risk of death rose below a BMI of 23 and above 33" (visible + FAQ + JSON-LD); `/blog/bmi-formula/` unsourced "A healthy PI is approximately 11–15 kg/m³" removed (homepage now says CDC and WHO publish no adult cut-offs for PI). Homepage draft fact-check findings also fixed before shipping: 170 cm pound range 118–158 lb (worked out on its own), body-fat calculator described as "estimate from tape or skinfolds" (not "directly"), 72.4% labelled as the sum of the two Health E-Stat 111 rates, all four WHO 2004 action points (23/27.5/32.5/37.5), Gallagher 2000 cited only to what its abstract states, underweight sentence quoted from Health E-Stat 119, Trefethen source added. **Registry & design**: 11 new rows in `research/data-sources.csv` (CDC child categories/About BMI/Calculating BMI, Lancet 2025, NIDDK Body Weight Planner, WHO TRS 854, WHO 2004, Winter 2014, Wing 2011, NHLBI 1998, Trefethen); `home` added to NHANES/NCHS/CDC rows it uses; `research/page-map.csv` `home` row set with `data_source` = `nhanes-2021-2023-exam-files` (`check_sources.py` now passes for `home`; 34 other rows still fail free-text `data_source`); `DESIGN.md` gets the one-decimal conversion rule, the rank rule and `.cat-dot`; `styles.css` gets `.cat-dot`. ### 2026-10-15 · CMB-013 HEALTHY-RANGE · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-013-healthy-range/` — `/blog/healthy-bmi-range/` rebuilt to the D-14 guide pattern (D-14 rollout #2). **Why**: guide owns 29 keywords worth 58,340/mo; old page drew an age-specific healthy range in its OG image (D-11), kept the backlog-P1 unsourced lines ("gray zone" 25–27, "lowest all-cause mortality 20–25", "0.5 BMI points per month"), misattributed the 2000 WPRO/IASO/IOTF proposal to WHO, misread Winter 2014, and promised a Lean Body Mass tab the homepage doesn't have (detector 15 at 390 / 31 at 1280). ### 2026-10-21 · CMB-016 BODY-FAT-CHART · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-016-body-fat-chart/` — `/body-fat-percentage-chart/` rebuilt to the D-14 guide pattern (D-14 rollout #5). **Why**: page owns 501 keywords worth 283,690/mo (`research/keyword-ledger.csv`: "body fat index" 74,000, "body fat percentage women" 27,100, "body fat percentage men" 18,100, "body fat percentage chart" 14,800 primary, the "N% body fat man/woman" long tail — "15 body fat men" 6,600, "20 percent bodyfat" 4,400, "30 body fat female" 3,600 and hundreds more). Old page had three page-level `<style>` blocks (one a tool with a gradient header), inline-styled containers, an FAQ with no FAQPage JSON-LD, an OG image with text and no preferred image (detector 8 at 390 / 17 at 1280). Owner's 2026-10-21 SERP pastes (US/English, not personalised: "body fat percentage chart", "body fat index", "body fat percentage women") show photo charts in image rows; AI Overviews quote the fitness-industry tiers (left out, D-11) and the provisional ranges by age. No result shows what US adults measure for every percentage, sets a reading against a real distribution, or shows how the provisional ranges sit against today's CDC scanner at the same BMI — that is the wedge. Owner's `bodyfatpercentagecalc.com` ranks at about position 16 for "body fat percentage women" (Search Console box in the paste). **Rebuild**: H1 "Body Fat Percentage Chart for Women and Men by Age" + byline → three intro paragraphs → takeaways → disclaimer → hero (the provisional ranges as banded bars for women/men 20–39, 40–59, 60–79) → 9 chips → the chart (`.weight-chart` six rows sex × age by low/healthy/high/very high + US median from CDC scans, `not scanned` for 60–79; row anchors `#women-20-39`…`#men-60-79`; Gallagher 2000 via J Public Health Afr 2016 Table 1, labelled provisional with ethnic-group caveat) → the rank tool (sex, body fat %, age 8–79 → share of US women/men with more body fat, interpolated between whole percent, provisional range for the age, percentile bar + age-group percentiles; under 20 percentiles only; 60–79 range only since CDC scanned ages 8–59) → every whole percent (two tables, men 5–40 and women 10–50, shares for US adults 20–39 and 40–59, tails print "more than 99%" / "under 1%"; anchors `#men-15`, `#women-30`) → healthy body fat section → body fat and BMI (median and middle half at each whole BMI, body fat in each BMI category, 5′4″/140 lb example; provisional ranges' BMI-25 edge on CDC scanner: adults within half a point of BMI 25 averaged 24.3% men 20–39 and 35.6% women 20–39, against the ranges' 20% and 33%) → age and sex (+2.9 points men and +3.4 points women from 20s to 50s, women 10.8–12.0 points above men every decade with 95% CI excluding 0; no scans over 59; percentile tables for every age group 8–59) → printable chart `assets/images/charts/body-fat-percentage-chart.png` (1360×902, cells in `bfpc_chart.csv`, `<img>` with alt+size+link, listed in image sitemap) → 10 FAQ (8 People-also-ask questions as asked + ledger's "average fat percentage" + old page's 60-and-over question; JSON-LD = visible text). **Head**: new `<title>` "Body Fat Percentage Chart: Women and Men by Age, US Averages" (60 chars; not "(CDC Data)" — CDC didn't make the ranges); new meta/OG/Twitter ("10–50% for women, 5–40% for men"); the preferred image; one JSON-LD graph with Article (H1 as headline, author and publisher named, image list, `mainEntityOfPage` with `primaryImageOfPage`, `dateModified`), Dataset (CSV download, creator named), BreadcrumbList (page name = H1) and FAQPage; three `<style>` blocks, visible breadcrumb and old OG image gone. **Images in Google results**: text-free preferred images in 16:9 (1200×675), 4:3 (1200×900) and 1:1 (1200×1200) from `research/preferred-images/pref_images.py` (new `body_fat` drawing; script now takes page names, so the other 12 files are not redrawn); page added to `PREFERRED` in `scripts/check_images.py`; sitemap `<image:image>` for the printable chart; old `og-body-fat-percentage-chart.png` deleted. **`styles.css`**: `.weight-chart--fit` (no 600 px minimum, cells may wrap; for three-column tables on 320 px); `.er-section h3` (results `h3` inside a section with its own `h2`); `.data-table .cell-kg` (muted second line in a data table); `.bfr …` sizes for the tool. All scoped — no other page uses them (checked across all 40 pages). **Registry & map**: no new rows; 7 rows list the page (`nhanes-2011-2018-dxa`, `gallagher-2000-bf-ranges`, `cdc-about-bmi`, `cdc-adult-bmi-categories`, `cdc-calculating-bmi`, `cdc-child-bmi-categories`, `body-fat-method-validation`); notes added on `nhanes-2011-2018-dxa` (CDC's documentation re-read 2026-10-21: ages 8–59, 450 lb/6′5″ exclusion, valid scans rarer at higher BMI, BCA option adding 5% of lean mass to fat mass) and `gallagher-2000-bf-ranges` (paper's own table still out of reach; abstract re-read); page-map row updated. **One-number fix elsewhere**: `/age-bmi-calculator/` printed men's 50s average 28.4% from `bf_results.json`'s 28.35 rounded again (unrounded 28.347) → 28.3%, plus its sitemap `lastmod` (no schema `dateModified` on that page). No other page prints a double-rounded body-fat figure (every page quoting `bf_results.json` checked). **Removed**: `research/body-fat/tool_test_jsdom.js` tested the old tool; `bfpc_test_playwright.py` covers the new one. **Build record**: `research/body-fat-chart-build/` (SPEC.md, `bfpc_stats.py`/`.json` stopping unless it reproduces `research/body-fat/bf_results.json` and the numbers on other pages, and stopping if storing a value would change any printed figure; `gen_bfpc.py`; `chart_bfpc.py` + `bfpc_chart.csv`; `bfpc_test_playwright.py`). Independent fact-check (separate agent, rebuilt from CDC's raw files, 59 strata, 121 PSUs, df 62, 10,864 adults): 2 FAILs fixed — double-rounding on men 50–59 (28.4 → 28.3; also on `/age-bmi-calculator/`), four lookup cells one point high (men 20–39 at 21% 78 → 77, men 40–59 at 31% 29 → 28, women 20–39 at 36% 62 → 61 and at 42% 27 → 26) and 22 of the tool's 882 whole-percent answers one point high; `bfpc_stats.py` now stores 4 decimals (tool 2–3) and stops if storing changes any figure. 4 WARNs fixed: BMI-25 cut-offs credited to the J Public Health Afr 2016 tabulation (not "the 2000 study" — paper's predictions differ by ethnic group); the fitness-tier sentence (those charts do name a source); "most cited" → "widely cited"; the DXA line now carries CDC's 5%-of-lean-mass adjustment. NOTEs taken (method wording, meta/OG range, noscript, title without "(CDC Data)", fat mass index, "measured" framing); the tie-order NOTE is the [P3] below, and the one tie-sensitive figure (the BMI gap) now prints whole points ("about 11 to 13" the same under every order).

### 2026-10-22 · CMB-017 WOMEN-CALC · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-017-women-calc/` — `/women-bmi-calculator/` rebuilt to the D-14 calculator pattern (D-14 rollout #6). **Why**: page owns 33 keywords worth 279,290/mo (`research/keyword-ledger.csv`: "bmi calculator women" 201,000 primary, "bmi calculate women" 74,000 with "womens bmi calculator" and "female bmi calculator", "calculate my bmi female" 880, "bmi chart women calculator" 720, "bmi older woman calculator" 390, "bmi calculator female asian" 210). Old page had inputs without `for`/`id`, had lost its key takeaways, disclaimer and weight-difference line in `a83d1f7`, carried unsourced "When to See a Doctor" lists and life-stage advice (menstrual cycle, postpartum, hormones, menopause), paraphrased Winter 2014 as "lowest mortality across BMI 23–33", cited ACOG pregnancy ranges with a text OG image, and used gradient purple cards with inline styles (detector 16 at 390 / 28 at 1280; with results open 16). Owner's 2026-10-22 SERP pastes (US/English, not personalised: "bmi calculator women", "bmi calculator female", "bmi calculator for older women") show every result is a bare calculator (CDC, NHLBI, Calculator.net, ACS, AARP, Harvard Health, Hers, Mayo's BMI-and-waist tool, Omni); the older-women AI Overview quotes a "65+" range of 23 to 29.9 from a fitness shop. No result places a woman among US women of her age, applies the June 2026 waist rule to her own waist, or says what body fat goes with her BMI — that is the wedge. **Rebuild**: H1 "BMI Calculator for Women" + byline → two intro lines → calculator (shared parts + `calculator.js`, inputs with `for`/`id`, optional waist input in or cm; on phones height pair and weight-and-waist pair side by side via `.form-row--pair`, Calculate ends at 761 px on 390×844 — 784 at 360, 852 at 320; scale labels use CDC names) → key takeaways → disclaimer → 10 chips → worked example (5′4″, 140 lb, waist 33 in; explains every output) → US women compared (BMI percentiles by age group table) → BMI by decade 20–29…80+ (figure + 4-column table: age, median, middle half, healthy; average 28.0 in 20s, 31.4 in 50s, 29.5 at 70–79, 27.7 at 80+, each difference outside its margin of error, different women measured in the same years; sample sizes in notes) → older women (CDC "regardless of age", senior calculators' 23–29.9 named as not CDC's, Winter 2014 worded to its abstract — "risk higher below 23", ADA's muscle note) → healthy weight at every height 4′10″–6′0″ with half the height (ratio 0.5; note where the ADA rule matters: BMI 25–29.9; 88 cm from 5′10″, 80 cm from 5′3″ for Asian background) → waist + the 2026 ADA standards (NICE ratio class, US guidelines' 35-in line, ADA 3.3a/3.3b non-Asian/Asian) → body fat at the same BMI (CDC DXA 2011–2018, women 20–59) → Asian and other backgrounds (CDC NDPP, WHO 2004, NICE NG246, ADA 2026) → CDC's pregnancy table → 10 FAQ (8 of 9 PAA questions as asked + ledger's "bmi for women formula" + old pregnancy question; the ninth PAA, "What is a good BMI for an older woman?", is answered by the 70-year-old and seniors questions; JSON-LD = visible). `/blog/bmi-chart-women/` keeps "What is a healthy BMI for a female?" (§5 [P3] settled). **Results panel (the wedge)**: shared answer-first panel + up to three flat `.rank-box` sections from the page script, each hidden when it has nothing to say — Compared with US women aged 20+ (rank by the US rank rule, plus 20–39, 40–59, 60+; NHANES 2021–2023, 3,249 women; all-women curve = homepage's, asserted equal); US women with a BMI like yours (DXA body fat at that whole BMI from CDC NHANES 2011–2018 women 20–59, plus share with a large waist in that BMI category); Waist check (only with a waist): NICE ratio class, US 35-inch line, 2026 ADA thresholds, with the Asian-background answer only when it differs. The waist check compares what it prints — waist to 0.1 in/cm, ratio from that waist and cut to 2 decimals (never rounded up) so 0.50 means at least half the height, BMI as shown; the US-guidelines line names only the unit entered; ADA sentences quote the waist in cm against 88 cm; "for women of non-Asian background" leads when the Asian-background answer differs; the source line says these are published thresholds applied to the numbers entered, not a diagnosis. **Head**: new `<title>` "BMI Calculator for Women: Waist Check and US Women Compared" (59 chars); new meta/OG/Twitter; the preferred image; one JSON-LD graph with WebPage (`primaryImageOfPage`), WebApplication (image list, feature list, author named), BreadcrumbList, FAQPage. **Images in Google results**: text-free preferred images `assets/images/preferred/women-bmi-calculator-{16x9,4x3,1x1}.png` (decade rows over the healthy band; the preferred image spans BMI 16.5–44.5, figure's axis 15–45) drawn by `research/preferred-images/pref_images.py` (new `women_calc` drawing; only this page's files redrawn); page added to `PREFERRED` in `scripts/check_images.py`; the page has no `<img>`, so no image-sitemap entry (as on `/`); old `women-acog-pregnancy-ranges.png` (card with text) deleted. **`styles.css`**: `.form-row.form-row--pair` keeps two columns below 600 px (this page only). **Registry & map**: no new rows; 13 rows list the page; notes added on `nhanes-2021-2023-exam-files`, `cdc-pregnancy-weight-gain` and `cdc-adult-bmi-categories` (both re-read 2026-10-22); `page-map.csv` row's `data_source` set to `nhanes-2021-2023-exam-files` so `check_sources.py` passes (FAIL 31 → 30). **Build record**: `research/women-calc-build/` (SPEC.md, `wbc_stats.py`/`.json` stopping unless the all-women curve equals `home_stats.json`, decade means equal `avgw_results.json` and age-group shares equal `bcw_stats.json`; stores 4 decimals, round once; `gen_wbc.py`; `wbc_test_playwright.py`). Independent fact-check (separate agent, its own code on CDC's raw files, page script run over 181,328 waist inputs): every number matched (396 curve points, percentile and decade tables, figure, averages and stated differences, 13 DXA cells rebuilt from raw files, waist shares, ADA shares, 60 healthy-weight bounds, worked example, FAQ JSON-LD; decade means and medians equal NCHS Series 3 No. 50). ### 2026-10-28 · CMB-019 AVERAGE-WEIGHT · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-019-average-weight/` — `/average-weight/` rebuilt to the D-14 guide-with-tool pattern (D-14 rollout #8). **Why**: the page owns 187 keywords worth 161,260/mo (`research/keyword-ledger.csv`: "average weight for men" 9,900 primary, "average weight 5 4 woman" 12,100, "average weight 5 7 female", "average weight for 5 9 male" and "average weight 5 5 female" 8,100 each; per-height phrasings, national averages, BMI, waist, by-age). The old page broke site rules — three page-level `<style>` blocks, 44 inline styles, visible inline-styled breadcrumb, single `<article>` wrapper, FAQ as `h3` without FAQPage JSON-LD, Organization+Person in the graph, text OG card, and "Weight peaks in middle age: men average the most at 40–49 (210.2 lb)" (no 30–59 step outside its margin of error, and NCHS prints 210.1); detector 10 at 390 / 19 at 1280. Owner's 2026-10-22 SERP pastes (US/English, not personalised: "average weight for men", "average weight 5 4 woman", "average weight for 5 9 male") show FastStats, Healthline, Hims, MNT, Rush, Banner, health.com, Verywell, AARP and Reddit; most quote 199.8 lb (CDC's 2015–2018 average); the AI Overview credits 199.8 lb to FastStats (which says 199.0) and gives the national average as the average at 5′9″; every height query is answered with a healthy-weight table or the national average. Wedge: no result says what Americans of a given height actually weigh. **Rebuild**: H1 "Average Weight for Men and Women by Height and Age" + byline → three intro paragraphs → key takeaways → disclaimer → hero figure (average weight at each height for women and men over the healthy band) → 8 chips → "How does your weight compare?" tool on the shared calculator parts (woman/man, US/metric, height, optional weight and age group — share of US women/men measured at that height ± 1 in who weigh less, box plot with the healthy range, BMI/category, age-group average, height rank; heights with fewer than 30 measured get the national average and healthy range) → women and men by height (one table per sex, one row per inch with 30+ measured — women 4′9″–5′9″, men 5′2″–6′3″; `#women-F-I` / `#men-F-I` row links; average with kg, middle half, healthy range at that height per the weight-table gate's rule, BMI 25+ share 60%–78% women and 64%–81% men, a † under 60 measured) → by age (= NCHS Table 4; stated changes only where 95% CI excludes 0 — +13.4 lb women and +10.2 lb men 30s-vs-20s; women lower each decade from 60; men 80+ 13.3 lb below their 70s; 30 to 59 "not statistically different") → height, BMI and waist (national means 68.9 / 63.5 in, BMI 29.4 / 30.0, waist 40.6 / 38.5 in; 47.4% of men and 67.5% of women over NHLBI's cm lines; BMI and waist by decade) → since 1960 (Advance Data No. 347 Table 6, ages 20–74 crude: 166.3 → 191.0 → 200.2 lb men, 140.2 → 164.3 → 173.7 lb women; our 2021–2023 calculation like-for-like) and 199.8 lb (NCHS Series 3 No. 46, 2015–2018) → 13 FAQ (national averages, 5′4″, 5′5″, 5′7″ women, 5′9″ men, three PAA as asked — "How heavy is a 5′7″ man?", "Is 200 pounds heavy for a male?", "Is 180 pounds at 5′9″ overweight?", average BMI, waist size, by age, and 199.8 lb; FAQPage JSON-LD = visible text) → sources & method → one related block. No `<article>` wrapper (D-16): sections sit directly in `main .container`. **Head**: title and meta description kept ("Average Weight for Men and Women by Height and Age (CDC)"), still true; new OG/Twitter text and `twitter:title`/`twitter:description`; the preferred images; JSON-LD Article (`dateModified` `{TODAY}`, author and publisher named, image list, `primaryImageOfPage`, citations), Dataset (`dateModified` `{TODAY}`, the CSV), BreadcrumbList, new FAQPage. Three `<style>` blocks and every inline style gone. **CSV** (`average-weight/average-weight-nhanes-2021-2023.csv`, same URL): regenerated with the new method; the old columns plus `bmi25_plus_pct`, `mean_bmi`, `se_bmi`, `mean_waist_in`, `se_waist_in` as the Dataset JSON-LD describes; medians and quartiles move by up to a few pounds, means don't. **Method / men's chart**: `research/average-weight/compute.py` now converts with 2.2046 lb/kg, reads percentiles from the weighted distribution over distinct measured values (no tie order, closes CMB-018 [P3]) and keeps pregnant women for height (as Table 8 does); stops unless it reproduces NCHS Series 3 No. 50's Table 4 in full and the adult rows of Tables 8, 10 and 12 (204 values). `avgw_results.json` adds what the page prints (by-height quartiles, BMI 25+ shares, tool's percentiles for the height and an inch either side, waist, ages 20–74, decade steps and 30–59 pairs with CIs). `research/chart-men-build/bcm_stats.py` uses the same method (its quartiles asserted equal to `avgw_results.json`), so `/blog/bmi-chart-men/` is regenerated: middle-half ranges and medians in its hero, by-height figure text and the 5′10″ answer move 0.1–1.8 lb (5′10″ median 199.3 → 199.1 lb, also in its FAQ); every other figure unchanged; `gen_bcm.py` asserts 199.1. Its preferred images redrawn; `dateModified` and sitemap `lastmod` to apply date. **Images in Google results**: text-free preferred images `assets/images/preferred/average-weight-{16x9,4x3,1x1}.png` (hero: healthy band + women's and men's averages per height, no words or numbers), drawn by `research/preferred-images/pref_images.py` (new `avg_weight` drawing; `men_chart` redrawn with the new quartiles); page joins `PREFERRED` in `scripts/check_images.py`; `average-weight/og-average-weight.png` deleted. **Registry & map**: 3 new rows (`cdc-faststats-body-measurements`, `nhanes-anthropometric-reference-2015-2018` for 199.8 / 170.8 lb, `nchs-advance-data-347` for Ogden 2004 Table 6); `average-weight` added to `pages_served` of `nhanes-anthropometric-reference`, `cdc-adult-bmi-categories` and `nhlbi-1998-obesity-guidelines` (7 rows list the page); notes on `nhanes-2021-2023-exam-files` and `nhanes-anthropometric-reference`. `page-map.csv` row's wedge and notes. **`styles.css`**: `.awt` shares the body-fat tool's three size rules (selectors added to the CMB-016 lines); `.awt [hidden] { display: none; }` (`.form-row`'s grid beats `hidden`); `.data-table--fit` (five-column number tables fit a 390 px phone — 13 px type, 6 px side padding below 600 px, numbers on one line). No other page uses these classes. **Dates**: both pages ship as `{TODAY}` templates for Article (and Dataset on `/average-weight/`) `dateModified`; the two sitemap `lastmod` are `{TODAY}` edits. **DESIGN.md**: "Average weight tool (CMB-019)". **Independent fact-check** (separate agent, own code on CDC's raw files, sources re-read live): no wrong number on either page; 498 published NCHS values reproduced (all of Tables 4, 8 and 12 and Table 10's adult rows, only with 2.2046 lb/kg), 27 by-height rows, hero, 14 decade means and 28 BMI/waist values, every stated and unstated age change, 13/13 FAQ (JSON-LD = visible), 43 CSV rows, tool on 4 inputs against raw shares (36%/36.2%, 57%/56.7%, 80%/80.3%, 41%/41.1%), and all 33 new values on `/blog/bmi-chart-men/`. Fixed before packaging — 6 WARNs: "differ by less than their margins of error" → "not statistically different" (every 30–59 pair's 95% CI includes zero; `compute.py` adds the 30s-vs-50s pair); the tool's "within an inch" → "measured at 5′8″ to 5′10″" / "an inch either side" (groups rounded heights h−1 to h+1); the waist sentence leads with NHLBI's cm lines, which the shares use; 200 lb at 6′3″ is "right at the line" (BMI 24.996), overweight runs 5′9″–6′2″; the CSV gains the BMI and waist columns the Dataset JSON-LD promised; "every height" → "every height measured (women 4′9″–5′9″, men 5′2″–6′3″)". NOTEs taken: method names exactly what is reproduced and cites Table 10 (BMI); the tool's percentiles are rounded once (126 of 2,673 were 0.1 lb off, nothing visible). NOTEs kept with reasons: rounded 18.5–24.9 rule (CMB-008), women's 30.0 "at the line" (NCHS prints 30.0), title's "(CDC)", "1960–1962" as No. 347 labels NHES I. ### 2026-10-28 · CMB-020 BODY-FAT-CALC · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-020-body-fat-calc/` — `/body-fat-calculator/` rebuilt to the D-14 calculator pattern (D-14 rollout #9). **Why**: page owns 329 keywords worth 147,710/mo (`research/keyword-ledger.csv`: "body fat calculator" 74,000 primary, "bfp calculator" 40,500 KWP's group, "body fat calculator online" 4,400; the methods — "bodyfat calculator caliper" 1,000, "7 site skinfold calculator" 320, "3 site skinfold calculator" 260, "relative fat mass calculator" 70; accuracy "most accurate body fat calculator" 390; sex 1,000/720; "body fat weight calculator" 210). Old page broke site rules — three page-level `<style>` blocks, inline styles in `<main>`, the single `<article>` wrapper, FAQ as `<details>`/`h3` without FAQPage JSON-LD, Organization and Person in the page graph, a text OG card, and a menu that marked "Obesity Statistics" as the current page (its shell came from `/us-obesity-statistics/`); detector 9 at 390 / 19 at 1280. Owner's 2026-10-28 SERP pastes (US/English, not personalised: "body fat calculator", "body fat percentage calculator", "body fat calculator female") show calculators that print a number from the Navy tape, BMI or skinfolds and stop there; none says how close that number is likely to be. That is the wedge. **Rebuild**: H1 "Body Fat Calculator" + byline → one-line intro and source note → the calculator → key takeaways → disclaimer → 9 chips → how to read your result (worked example + figure) → how accurate is each method (list, paired comparison, error figure, one three-column table per formula, published studies) → tape measure → waist and height → height and weight → calipers → what your result means (provisional Gallagher ranges, one table per sex, ranges as rows) → 9 FAQ (ledger-owned queries as questions: most accurate, height-and-weight, relative fat mass, where to measure waist, 3-vs-7, pounds of fat, women, teenagers, cm; JSON-LD = visible) → sources & method → one related block. No `<article>` wrapper (D-16); no page-level `<style>`; no inline style in `<main>`. **The calculator** (shared parts, `.calculator-card.bfc`): woman/man, US/metric, four methods — tape measure = Navy equations; waist and height = relative fat mass (Woolcott & Bergman 2018); height and weight = Deurenberg; calipers = Jackson–Pollock 3 or 7 sites with Siri. Answer first — estimate on a card tinted by the Gallagher range (neutral under 20), then how close the method is likely to be, where it falls among US adults of the same sex and age (the `/body-fat-percentage-chart/` figure and data), fat and lean mass from the weight, the other estimates the entered numbers allow, and the source line. Notes say what each check can and can't tell (CDC's measurements by trained technicians; the US share and the range treat the estimate as a scan reading). US/Metric switch converts to 0.1 and restores entered numbers when switched back with nothing retyped. **The wedge** (our calculation, CDC NHANES 2011–2018 DXA, 10,864 adults 20–59, survey-weighted, 95% CIs): waist-and-height within 5 points of the scan for 74.3% of women / 86.3% of men, height-and-weight 62.4% / 66.4%; same people, 12 more women and 20 more men in 100 (CIs exclude 0); typical miss 3.5 / 2.7 vs 4.7 / 4.2 (all four CIs exclude zero); waist-and-height reads high (+2.3 / +0.7 on average; median error +2.3 / +0.8 against the authors' 0.9 / 0.5 — clearly higher for women, not clearly different for men); height-and-weight is right on average (−0.2 / −0.1) only because it reads 3.1 / 2.9 low at healthy BMI and 4.1 / 2.9 high with obesity, about 2 low under 40 and 2 high from 40 to 59; for any estimate from those two formulas the calculator shows the middle half and median of the scans of US adults whose formula gave the same whole percent ± 1 (30 or more people); tape and calipers can't be checked on CDC's data (no neck or skinfolds), so the page cites Foulis 2023 (1,407 Army recruits: 6.0 points below DXA) and Burns 2019 (437 students: 7-site skinfolds 1.4 points below DXA on average, lowest MAPE 11.7%). **`/body-fat-percentage-chart/` fix**: rank tool's "You" label was 0.5–0.9 px above its figure, so the top clipped — every mark moves 2 units down and the figure is 88 units tall (the CMB-019 `/average-weight/` fix). One edit on the page's script; `research/body-fat-chart-build/gen_bfpc.py` matches. No visible text, link or JSON-LD changes, so no date moves. **Head**: title "Body Fat Calculator: 4 Methods Compared With DXA Scans"; new meta/OG/Twitter (OG says CDC's scans checked two formulas and published studies the tape and calipers); the preferred images; JSON-LD `@graph`: WebPage (`dateModified` `{TODAY}`, author, `primaryImageOfPage`), WebApplication `#calculator` (image list, featureList, author), BreadcrumbList, FAQPage. The menu now marks Body Fat as the current page. **Images in Google results**: text-free preferred images `body-fat-calculator-{16x9,4x3,1x1}.png` (accuracy figure: formula minus scan for both formulas and both sexes on a dashed zero line, no words or numbers) from `pref_images.py` (new `bf_calc`); page added to `PREFERRED`; `body-fat-calculator/og-body-fat-calculator.png` deleted. **Registry & map**: 3 new rows (`rfm-woolcott-2018`, `foulis-2023-circumference-dxa`, `army-tape-worksheets`); page added to `pages_served` of `nhanes-2011-2018-dxa`, `gallagher-2000-bf-ranges` and `body-fat-method-validation`; notes on `body-fat-equations` (Topend Sports added as the JP-coefficients endpoint; Deurenberg abstract re-read), `body-fat-method-validation` (Burns: 7 sites, Siri, Table 3 sign — skinfolds read 1.4 below DXA) and `nhanes-2011-2018-dxa` (the CMB-020 check, exact ties, percentile rule, CDC's "trained health technicians"); `rfm-woolcott-2018` carries Table 2's CIs and our median CIs; `army-tape-worksheets` Army Directive 2023-11. Page-map wedge and notes (`data_source` stays `body-fat-equations`). **`styles.css`**: `.bfc` shares the body-fat rank tool's three size rules; `.bfc [hidden]`; `.bfc-toggles` (sex and units side by side, stacked under 360 px); `.unit-toggle--methods` (four options, two rows on phones); `.form-row--trio` (three measurements in a row, two under 360 px); `.bfc .form-hint`; below 600 px 8 px beside a `th[colspan]`. No other page uses these classes. **Dates**: page ships as `{TODAY}` template for WebPage `dateModified`; sitemap `lastmod` is a `{TODAY}` edit. `/body-fat-percentage-chart/` keeps its dates. **DESIGN.md**: "Body fat calculator (CMB-020)". **Independent fact-check** (separate agent, own code on CDC's raw files, Playwright, sources re-read live): 1 FAIL, 6 WARN, 12 NOTE, all settled before packaging. Passed: every sample count (10,864; 10,751; 10,829; 59 strata, 121 PSUs), 35 of 36 table values, all 44 accuracy-figure values, every margin-of-error statement, the paired comparison, the US means, all 137 calibration windows, all 630 US-comparison shares (= `bfpc_stats.json`), the calculator in 24 runs, the sources (Foulis, Burns, Deurenberg, Woolcott, CDC's DXX_J, DA 5500 and 5501), FAQ JSON-LD 9/9, links. FAIL (fixed): float noise decided ties — a Deurenberg estimate is an exact hundredth, 6 women whose error was exactly 3.00 fell outside "within 3 points"; `bfc_stats.py` now rounds Deurenberg estimates and errors to 0.01 (RFM to 10 decimals), within 3 points for women 41.4% → 41.5%; 30 of 81 windows change count by 1–4. WARNs (fixed): OG overclaimed (now CDC's scans checked two formulas, published studies the tape and calipers); the Woolcott comparison now gives Woodruff median CIs with "cause unknown"; Burns's 1.4 is signed (now "read 1.4 points below DXA on average" with MAPE 11.7% named beside it); unit switch changed the result (now 0.1 conversions that round-trip); 0.0001392 coefficient cites Topend Sports (Serra named as the 0.0001395 variant); the results overstate precision (added "trained technicians", "measuring yourself at home can add more error", and the US share and range "treat this estimate as a scan reading"). NOTEs (fixed): one percentile rule on the page (calibration windows use the chart's weighted midpoint rule over distinct values; within 0.08 of the chart's 50 printed quartiles), "standard error of estimate", waist site cites Cedars-Sinai and Woolcott-Bergman, "all four differences", 2013 worksheets + Army Directive 2023-11, FAQ for men's navel site cites DA 5500 and 5501, both Burns and Foulis cited for "most accurate", smaller wording fixes; "less than 0.1 point, even at 60" → "less than 0.02 points at any age from 18 to 79". Kept with reasons: 18/19-year-olds compared with US "girls"/"boys" aged 16–19 (chart tool); Table B-5 and the J Public Health Afr table couldn't be re-read live but earlier batches verified them.
