### 2026-10-29 · CMB-021 HOW-TO-MEASURE · chat → CC · done
- What: applied `research/cmb-021-how-to-measure/` — `/blog/how-to-measure-body-fat/` rebuilt to the D-14 guide pattern (D-14 rollout #10). **Why**: page owns 913 keywords worth 114,170/mo (`research/keyword-ledger.csv`: "how to calculate body fat percentage" 18,100 primary; "how to figure body fat" and "how to check fat percentage" 6,600 each; scales 8.1k; at home 4.3k; calipers 2.4k; tape 1.1k; InBody 670). Old page broke site rules — three page-level `<style>` blocks, 10 inline styles in `<main>`, the single `<article>` wrapper, 5-question FAQ as `<details>`/`h3` without FAQPage JSON-LD, Organization and Person in the page graph, a text OG card, menu marked "Obesity Statistics" as current; detector 7 at 390 / 18 at 1280. Old numbers told half the story: its chart drew five of the seven Burns 2019 methods as distances from DXA ("within 1.0–1.6 points") though Table 3 is signed (every method read lower than the scan); two methods had "Not reported in text" though Table 3 reports them; the tape method rested on a conference abstract. Owner's 2026-10-29 SERP pastes: AI Overviews list the methods, quote ACE tiers (D-11) and give scales "21% to 34%"; "Is 30% body fat chubby?" in every People-also-ask. **Rebuild**: D-14 — H1 "How to Calculate Body Fat Percentage" (kept) → byline → 3-paragraph intro → key takeaways (answer + 5 bullets) → disclaimer → hero figure → 8 chips → how to calculate at home → how close tested methods get to a DXA scan → how accurate are body fat scales → how to measure accurately → lab and gym methods → what your number means → 10 FAQ → sources & method → one related block. No `<article>` wrapper (D-16); no page-level `<style>`; no inline style in `<main>` outside figures; no visible dates (D-12); no fitness tiers (D-11). **Steps, worked by hand for one man** (40, 5′10″, 180 lb, neck 15.5 in, waist 36 in at the navel and 37 in at the top of the hip bones, skinfolds 15 / 25 / 18 mm): relative fat mass 26.2%, Navy tape 20.4%, Deurenberg 24.0%, JP 3-site with Siri 18.5% (density 1.0565; page says no study it cites checked the 3-site version against DXA). Measurements follow Cedars-Sinai and Woolcott & Bergman (waist at top of hip bones), the Army's 2013 worksheets (DA 5500 and 5501) and Burns et al. (two readings, a third if they differ by more than 2 mm). **Wedge** (hero figure draws all seven Burns 2019 methods on 437 students against one scanner, method minus DXA with 90% CI; our calc on CDC NHANES 2011–2018 DXA, 10,864 adults 20–59, survey-weighted): every Burns method read low on average, 1.0 (underwater) to 4.9 (hand-held) points; MAPE 11.7% (7-site skinfolds) to 21.9%; equivalence margin ±2.7 crossed by underwater weighing, skinfolds, the Bod Pod and the four-electrode analyzer; our two formulas miss by 3.1 (RFM) and 4.4 (Deurenberg) points on average — 10.4% and 14.0% of the scan — but those shares aren't comparable with Burns's because CDC's average 32.9% body fat vs the students' 27.0%. The formulas disagree for the same person: more than 5 points apart for 38% of US women and 15% of men (median gap 4.1 / 2.4), drawn as a formula-gap figure (within 3 / 3–5 / more than 5: women 37/25/38%, men 60/25/15%). A 1-inch waist slip moves RFM by 0.9 points for the typical US adult; 5 lb on the scale 0.9–1.0 on Deurenberg; height 1 in short 1.0–1.1; tape 1.2–1.9; 2 mm at each skinfold site 1.7–1.9. Scales/clinic: CDC's impedance docs (hand-and-foot electrodes, exclusions, 6-hr fast); Frija-Masson 2021 (three smart scales, weight within 0 to 0.3 kg, fat mass 2.2 to 4.4 kg low); Lahav 2021 (InBody 770 and SECA mBCA 515); Foulis 2023 (tape 6.0 below DXA, 1,407 recruits); DXA itself (CDC: most widely accepted; under 20 µSv; 5% lean-to-fat option; 450 lb and 6′5″ limits). **FAQ**: 10 (ledger-owned queries): at home, most accurate, scale accuracy, how scales measure body fat, calipers, tape measure, InBody, Bod Pod, why methods differ, how much body fat; JSON-LD = visible. **Head**: title "How to Calculate Body Fat Percentage: Home Methods vs DXA"; new meta/OG/Twitter; JSON-LD Article (`@id` and `datePublished` 2026-10-07 kept, `dateModified` `{TODAY}`, author/publisher named, image list, `primaryImageOfPage`, citations), BreadcrumbList (kept), FAQPage. Menu now marks Guides in both menus. Three page-level `<style>` blocks, 10 inline styles, `<article>` wrapper, Org/Person graph gone. **Images in Google results**: text-free preferred images `how-to-measure-body-fat-{16x9,4x3,1x1}.png` (hero: seven dots with their interval lines on dashed zero lines, no words or numbers) from `pref_images.py` new `htm` drawing; page added to `PREFERRED`; `blog/how-to-measure-body-fat/og-how-to-measure-body-fat.png` deleted. **Registry & map**: 3 new rows (`frija-masson-2021-smart-scales`, `lahav-2021-inbody-seca`, `nhanes-bia-documentation`); 6 rows gain the page (`nhanes-2011-2018-dxa`, `rfm-woolcott-2018`, `foulis-2023-circumference-dxa`, `army-tape-worksheets`, `body-fat-equations`, `gallagher-2000-bf-ranges`); notes on `body-fat-method-validation` (all seven Table 3 rows; WKU conference-abstract endpoint no longer cited; devices typed in: Valhalla 1990B, Futrex on biceps, Tanita BF-556, Omron HBF-306) and `nhanes-2011-2018-dxa` (the CMB-021 numbers; DXX_J re-read); Topend Sports' Siri page added to `body-fat-equations`' endpoints (prints 495 ÷ density − 450). No result says what each of those numbers counts. **Rebuild**: H1 "US Obesity Rate and Statistics" (kept) + byline → three intro paragraphs → key takeaways → disclaimer → hero figure (the 60-year measured trend from HE-Stat 111 Table 1: obesity 13.4% → 40.8%, severe obesity 0.9% → 10.2%, overweight 30.3% to 34.4%; 2017–2018 highest estimate at 42.8%, not significantly above 2021–2023 — never "peak") → 10 chips → how many Americans are obese (category figure in millions; six-category table with class 1 20.6% / class 2 10.3% / class 3 10.3% from CDC's 2021–2023 files; "What % overweight?") → where do you fit (the tool on shared calculator parts, `.calculator-card.obw`) → US obesity rate by year (all 15 surveys) → rising or falling (NHANES 2021–2023 vs 2017–2018 not significantly different at 2.13 × SE; BRFSS 2024→2025 21 states at 35%+ vs 19) → why do sources say 37%, 40%, 42% or 75% (the wedge table) → age/gender/education → race and Hispanic origin (CDC prints flagged estimates as "n/a*"; table drops the only em dashes) → severe obesity → children → use this chart or data → 12 FAQ (5 People-also-ask first, then 68% / 75%, 42.4% / 41.9%, average BMI, age, sex, 300 lb at 2.9%, healthy weight; JSON-LD = visible text) → sources & method → one related block.
